<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>X</i> be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let <i>B</i> be an effective rational simple normal crossing divisor with coefficients less than one, and let <i>M</i> be a nef rational holomorphic line bundle on <i>X</i>. If $`K_X+B`$ is pseudo-effective and $`K_X+B+M`$ is nef, we prove that its first Chern class in real Bott–Chern cohomology is represented by a semiample rational line bundle. This numerical statement allows a flat change of line bundle; it does not assert semiampleness of the original adjoint.

- **claim-main-numerical-semiample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let $B\geq0$ be a rational simple normal crossing divisor whose coefficients are strictly less than one, and let $M\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$. Assume that $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, $M$ is nef, and $D=K_X+B+M$ is nef. Then there is a semiample rational line bundle $L$ on $X$ such that $$c_1(L)=c_1(D)\quad\text{in }H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(X,\mathbb R).$$
  - Explanation: For a klt pair on a compact Kähler manifold, a nef adjoint with a nef summand has the Chern class of a semiample rational line bundle. This is a numerical statement; the original adjoint need not be semiample.
- **claim-decomposition-induction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-decomposition)): Let $T$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, $B\geq0$ a rational simple normal crossing divisor with coefficients less than one, and $M$ a nef rational line bundle. Suppose $J=K_T+B$ is pseudo-effective. There are a smooth compact Kähler modification $\mu:U\to T$, a semiample rational line $P$ on $U$, and an effective rational divisor $R$ on $U$, such that $$\mu^*(J+M)\equiv P+R,\qquad R=N\bigl(c_1(\mu^*(J+M))\bigr).$$ Here $\equiv$ denotes equality of real Chern classes. Moreover, if $a(T)=0$, then $c_1(M)=0$.
  - Explanation: After a modification, the adjoint plus a nef summand splits numerically into a semiample part and its analytic negative part; the nef summand vanishes when the algebraic dimension is zero.
- **claim-lines-zero-class** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-picard)): Let $\mu:Y\to X$ be a modification between smooth compact Kähler manifolds.* 1. *A rational line whose real Chern class is zero belongs to $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$.* 2. *Pullback identifies $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X)$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(Y)$.* 3. *If a rational line $L$ on $X$ has semiample pullback, then $L$ is semiample.
  - Explanation: Rational lines with zero real Chern class lie in the rationalised identity component of the Picard group, which is invariant under modifications; semiampleness of a pullback descends.
- **claim-negative-part-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-negative)): Let $\alpha$ be pseudo-effective on a smooth compact Kähler manifold.* 1. *Every positive current in $\alpha$ contains $[N(\alpha)]$. If $0\leq F\leq N(\alpha)$, then $$N(\alpha-\{F\})=N(\alpha)-F.$$* 2. *If $\beta$ is nef, then $N(\alpha+\beta)\leq N(\alpha)$. In particular $N(\beta)=0$.* 3. *A modified nef class restricts pseudo-effectively to a resolution of every prime divisor.* 4. *If $\mu:Y\to X$ is a smooth modification and $\alpha=\beta+\{R\}$, with $\beta$ nef and $R=N(\alpha)$, then $$N(\mu^*\alpha)=\mu^*R.$$* 5. *Suppose $\mu:Y\to X$ is a modification from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a normal compact Kähler space, $A$ is a rational line on $X$, and $E\geq0$ is $\mu$-exceptional. If $\mu^*A+E$ is pseudo-effective, then $\mu^*A$ is pseudo-effective and $$N(\mu^*A+E)=N(\mu^*A)+E.$$
  - Explanation: Rules for the analytic negative part of a pseudo-effective class: containment in positive currents, subtraction of divisors, behaviour under nef additions, restriction to prime divisors, and pullback and exceptional translation.
- **claim-ordinary-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-ordinary)): Let $X$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold, and let $B$ be a rational simple normal crossing divisor with coefficients in $[0,1]$. If $J=K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, there is a smooth compact Kähler modification $\mu:U\to X$ and an identity $$\mu^*J\sim_{\mathbb Q}P+R,\qquad P\text{ semiample},\qquad R=N(\mu^*J)\geq0,$$ where $P$ is a rational line and $R$ is a rational divisor.
  - Explanation: For a pseudo-effective log canonical line on a compact Kähler manifold, an actual rational-line identity gives a semiample part plus the analytic negative part on some modification.
- **claim-contraction-negative-part** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-contract)): Let $(X,B)$ be a smooth compact Kähler klt pair with rational simple normal crossing boundary. Suppose $$K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}P+R,\qquad P\text{ semiample},\qquad R=N(K_X+B)\geq0$$ with $P,R$ rational. A finite ordinary $(K_X+B)$-negative program reaches a normal compact Kähler klt model on which the log canonical line is semiample. Its steps contract or flip detected analytic extremal rays, are $P$-trivial, and preserve a fixed generated Cartier multiple of $P$ as an actual line. The final adjoint is rationally linearly equivalent to the descended semiample line. The steps extract no divisors; on a common resolution the initial adjoint equals the pullback of the final adjoint plus an effective divisor exceptional over the final model.
  - Explanation: When the negative part is known, a finite negative program reaches a klt Kähler model where the adjoint is semiample, with steps trivial against the semiample part and extracting no divisors.
- **claim-projective-positive-parts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-projective)): Let $(S,\Delta)$ be a normal projective klt pair with rational boundary, and let $M$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor. Suppose $K_S+\Delta$ is pseudo-effective. There is a smooth projective modification $u:S'\to S$, a birational morphism $v:S'\to S_m$ to a normal projective variety, a nef rational Cartier divisor $H_m$ on $S_m$, and an effective rational $v$-exceptional divisor $E$, such that $$\label{pre:projective-model} u^*(K_S+\Delta+M)\sim_{\mathbb Q}v^*H_m+E.$$ On a sufficiently high such model, $v^*H_m$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample rational divisor and $$N\bigl(u^*(K_S+\Delta+M)\bigr)=E.$$
  - Explanation: Projective input: for a projective klt pair with a nef summand, a model carries a nef divisor plus an exceptional effective divisor that is the negative part, with the nef part numerically semiample.
- **claim-numerical-descent-nef** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-nef)): Let $f:X\to Y$ be a fibration of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, and let $M\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes\mathbb Q$ be analytically nef. Suppose that $c_1(M\|_{X_y})=0$ on a general smooth fiber. There are smooth compact Kähler modifications $p:X'\to X$ and $q:Y'\to Y$, a fibration $g:X'\to Y'$ satisfying $fp=qg$, and a rational line $N$ on $Y'$ such that $$\label{desc:numerical-identity} p^*M\equiv g^*N.$$ If $Y$ is projective, $Y'$ can be chosen projective and $N$ is nef.
  - Explanation: A nef rational line with zero class on general fibers descends numerically to a rational line on a modified base, nef when the base is projective.
- **claim-descended-representative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-representative)): In the notation and under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#desc:nef" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:nef">8</a>, there is a rational line $M^*$ on $X$ with $M^*\equiv M$ such that $$p^*M^*\sim_{\mathbb Q}g^*N.$$
  - Explanation: A single flat twist on the source turns numerical descent into an actual rational line identity.
- **claim-flat-line-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-flat)): Let $f:X\to Y$ be a fibration of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, and let $F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X)\otimes\mathbb Q$. If $F$ is trivial as a rational line on a general smooth fiber, then $$F\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*G$$ for some $G\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(Y)\otimes\mathbb Q$.
  - Explanation: A flat rational line that is trivial on a general fiber comes from a flat rational line on the base.
- **claim-adjoint-on-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-adj-base)): Let $(Y,D)$ be an effective klt pair with rational boundary on a normal compact Kähler space. Let $f:Y\to Z$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers to a normal projective variety, and suppose that $\dim Y>\dim Z$. If $H$ is a rational Cartier line bundle on $Z$ and $$\label{adj:initial} K_Y+D\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*H,$$ then there is an effective rational divisor $D_Z$ such that $(Z,D_Z)$ is klt and $$H\sim_{\mathbb Q}K_Z+D_Z.$$
  - Explanation: If an adjoint on a klt Kähler pair is pulled back from a projective base, the line downstairs is itself an adjoint of a klt pair on the base.
- **claim-period-quotient-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-adj-period-input)): Let $S$ be a smooth projective variety and let $S^\circ\subset S$ have simple normal crossing complement. Let $\mathbb V$ be a real-polarizable pure variation on $S^\circ$ with an integral lattice and quasi-unipotent local monodromy. Suppose that a complex direct summand of $\mathbb V_{\mathbb C}$, as a variation of Hodge structures, has a rank-one highest Hodge piece, with parabolic extension $L$. Then $L$ is nef, and its numerical dimension is the generic rank of its line period map.* *After modification $\tau:S'\to S$, there are a smooth projective variety $Q$, a surjective morphism $p:S'\to Q$ with connected fibers, and a nef rational line bundle $P$ on $Q$ such that $$\label{adj:period-descent} \tau^*L\sim_{\mathbb Q}p^*P.$$ Moreover, $\dim Q$ is at most the generic rank of the full period map of $\mathbb V$. If $Q$ is a point, $\tau^*L$ is rationally trivial.
  - Explanation: The parabolic extension of a rank-one highest Hodge piece is nef, its numerical dimension is the generic rank of the line period map, and it descends from a quotient whose dimension is controlled by the full period rank.
- **claim-root-eigenline** (theorem, [source](#stmt-adj-root)): In the setting of Proposition <a href="#adj:base" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="adj:base">11</a>, there is a dense Zariski open $S^\circ\subset S$ and a smooth proper family $h:V^\circ\to S^\circ$ obtained from a cyclic cover and resolution, such that $R^l h_*\mathbb R$, where $l=\dim Y-\dim Z$, is real-polarizable and has an integral lattice. One character summand of its complexification has a rank-one highest Hodge piece. On $S^\circ$, this line agrees, as a rational line bundle, with $b^*H-K_S$.
  - Explanation: A cyclic cover gives a polarizable variation with a rank-one highest Hodge piece agreeing generically with the target moduli line.
- **claim-extension-discriminant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-adj-extension)): The parabolic extension of the eigenline in Lemma <a href="#adj:root" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="adj:root">13</a> is $L_S$ of <a href="#adj:discriminant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="adj:discriminant">[adj:discriminant]</a>. This identification persists on higher smooth base models with simple normal crossing complement.
  - Explanation: Across the discriminant, the parabolic extension of the eigenline is the moduli line, also on higher smooth models.
- **claim-period-rank-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-adj-rank)): For the family of Lemma <a href="#adj:root" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="adj:root">13</a>, the line period map of its root eigenline and the full period map of $R^lh_*\mathbb R$ have the same generic differential rank.
  - Explanation: The line period map and the full period map have the same generic rank, shown by lifting vector fields in the kernel to the cover.
- **claim-zero-dimension-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-vanishing)): Let $n\ge1$. Assume that the algebraic-dimension-zero assertion of Theorem <a href="#thm:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:decomposition">2</a> holds in every dimension less than $n$. Let $T$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler $n$-fold with $a(T)=0$, and let $B$ be an effective rational simple normal crossing boundary with coefficients less than one. If $K_T+B$ is pseudo-effective, then every nef rational holomorphic line bundle $M$ on $T$ satisfies $c_1(M)=0$.
  - Explanation: In algebraic dimension zero, under the inductive hypothesis, every nef rational line has zero class.
- **claim-mass-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-mass-test)): Let $V$ be a smooth compact Kähler $n$-fold, where $n\ge1$, and let $\gamma$ be a nef class, and let $C$ be a nef and big class. If $\gamma\cdot C^{n-1}=0$, then $\gamma=0$. In particular, if $V$ admits a generically finite surjective morphism to a complex torus of algebraic dimension zero, every nef rational line class on $V$ is zero.
  - Explanation: A nef class with zero intersection against a power of a nef and big class vanishes, so nef lines vanish on manifolds mapping generically finitely to a torus of algebraic dimension zero.
- **claim-geometric-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-geometric-reduction)): Under the induction hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#zero:vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="zero:vanishing">16</a>, it suffices to prove the following assertion: every nef rational line class on a smooth simple compact Kähler manifold of algebraic dimension zero carrying a generically nondegenerate holomorphic two-form is zero.
  - Explanation: The vanishing reduces to simple symplectic Kähler manifolds of algebraic dimension zero.
- **claim-no-correspondences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-no-correspondences)): Let $X$ be a smooth simple compact Kähler manifold of algebraic dimension zero, carrying a generically nondegenerate holomorphic two-form. Suppose that every smooth connected compact Kähler manifold mapping generically finitely onto $X$ has irregularity zero. Through a very general point of $X^2$, the only positive-dimensional proper irreducible compact analytic subvarieties are the two factor slices.
  - Explanation: On the square of such a manifold, the only proper positive-dimensional subvarieties through a very general point are the factor slices.
- **claim-cotangent-slope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-cotangent-slope)): Let $X$ be a smooth simple compact Kähler manifold of dimension $n\ge2$, algebraic dimension zero and irregularity zero, and suppose $K_X$ is pseudo-effective. Let $\mathscr L$ be a holomorphic line bundle such that $L=c_1(\mathscr L)\ge c_1(K_X)$ in pseudo-effective order. For every nonzero map $\mathscr H\to\Omega_X^{\otimes k}$, where $\mathscr H$ is a line bundle and $k\ge0$, $$\label{zero:slope-X} c_1(\mathscr H)\le kL.$$ For every very general $x\in X$, write $a:Y=\operatorname{Bl}_xX\to X$. Every nonzero line map $\mathscr H_Y\to(a^*\Omega_X)^{\otimes k}$ satisfies $$\label{zero:slope-blowup} c_1(\mathscr H_Y)\le ka^*L.$$ The exceptional set of points can be chosen simultaneously for all $\mathscr H_Y$ and $k$.
  - Explanation: Line subsheaves of cotangent tensors satisfy a slope bound by the chosen pseudo-effective class, also on the blowup at a very general point.
- **claim-line-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-line-nonvanishing)): Let $X$ be a smooth simple compact Kähler manifold of dimension $n\ge2$, with $a(X)=0$ and irregularity zero. Assume that the only positive-dimensional proper compact irreducible analytic subvarieties through a very general point of $X^2$ are its factor slices. Let $\mathscr L$ be a holomorphic line bundle with pseudo-effective class $L$. Assume the following line-subsheaf bounds:* 1. *For every line bundle $\mathscr H$, every integer $k\ge0$, and every nonzero map $\mathscr H\to\Omega_X^{\otimes k}$, one has $c_1(\mathscr H)\le kL$.* 2. *Outside a countable union of proper analytic subsets of $X$, every point $x$ has this property: if $a:Y=\operatorname{Bl}_xX\to X$, then for every line bundle $\mathscr H_Y$, every $k\ge0$, and every nonzero map $\mathscr H_Y\to(a^*\Omega_X)^{\otimes k}$, one has $c_1(\mathscr H_Y)\le ka^*L$.* *Then some positive power of $\mathscr L$ has a nonzero meromorphic section.
  - Explanation: Cotangent slope bounds and the absence of correspondences force a power of the line bundle to have a nonzero meromorphic section.
- **claim-pole-threshold** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-pole-threshold)): Let $W$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $e\ge2$, $\alpha$ a big real $(1,1)$-class, and $z\in W$. Suppose a smooth projective modification $\mu:W'\to W$, which is an isomorphism near $z$, admits a decomposition $$\mu^*\alpha=K+\{D\},\qquad K\ \text{Kähler},\quad D\ge0,$$ where $D$ misses the point $z'$ over $z$. If the ordinary analytic Seshadri constant $\epsilon(K,z')$ is at least $\eta>0$, then, on the blowup $b:\widehat W=\operatorname{Bl}_zW\to W$ with exceptional divisor $G$, $$\label{zero:pole-formula} \sup\{u\ge0:b^*\alpha-u\{G\}\ge0\} \le \frac{\eta}{2} +2^{e-1}\operatorname{vol}(\alpha)\eta^{-(e-1)} .$$
  - Explanation: A Seshadri-type lower bound on the Kähler part bounds the point pole threshold in terms of volume.
- **claim-bundle-subvarieties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-subvarieties)): For the projective bundle $Z$ just defined, the only positive-dimensional compact irreducible analytic subvarieties through a very general point are a fiber of $\pi$, the full inverse images of the two factor slices, and $Z$ itself.
  - Explanation: Through a very general point of the projective bundle only a fiber, the inverse images of the two slices and the whole bundle occur as positive-dimensional subvarieties.
- **claim-two-slot-volume** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-two-slot-volume)): For these choices, $M$ is big and $$\label{zero:M-bounds} c_nq\le\operatorname{vol}(M)\le C_nq,\qquad \tau(M,z):=\sup\{u\ge0:b_z^*M-u\{F_z\}\ge0\}\le C_nA_q$$ at a very general point $z\in Z$, where $b_z:\operatorname{Bl}_zZ\to Z$ has exceptional divisor $F_z$.
  - Explanation: The constructed class is big with volume growing at the scale of the parameter and a slowly growing point pole threshold.
- **claim-nef-pushforward** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-nef-pushforward)): Let $f:U\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\dim Z_0=b_0\ge1$ and $\dim U=b_0+e$, where $e\ge1$. Let $\beta$ be a Kähler class, $w=\int_{U_z}\beta^e$ its general fiber volume, and $$B_0=\frac{f_*\beta^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}.$$ There are smooth projective modifications $p:Z'_0\to Z_0$ and $q:U'\to U$, and a morphism $f':U'\to Z'_0$ with $pf'=fq$, such that $$B'_0:=\frac{f'_*(q^*\beta)^{e+1}}{(e+1)w} =p^*B_0-\{D_0\}$$ is nef and $D_0$ is an effective $p$-exceptional real divisor.
  - Explanation: After flattening, the pushforward class of a Kähler class is nef up to an exceptional effective divisor.
- **claim-direct-image-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-direct-image)): Let $f:U\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\dim Z_0=b_0\ge1$ and $\dim U=b_0+e$, $e\ge1$. Suppose $$\beta=f^*G+c_1(\Lambda)$$ is a Kähler class, where $G\in H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(Z_0,\mathbb R)$ and $\Lambda\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(U)\otimes\mathbb Q$. Put $$w=\int_{U_z}\beta^e>0$$ on a general fiber. For sufficiently large divisible integers $j$, let $\mathcal F_j=f_*\mathcal O_U(j\Lambda)$ and $R_j=\operatorname{rk}\mathcal F_j$. Here $j\Lambda$ is an actual line bundle and $c_1(\mathcal F_j)$ means $c_1(\det\mathcal F_j)$. Then $$\begin{aligned} R_j&=\frac{w}{e!}j^e+O(j^{e-1}),\label{zero:GRR-rank}\\ B_0:=G+\lim_j\frac{c_1(\mathcal F_j)}{jR_j} &=\frac{f_*\beta^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}\ge0,\label{zero:GRR-base}\\ \int_U\beta^{b_0+e} &\le (e+1)^{b_0}w\,\operatorname{vol}(B_0).\label{zero:mixed-bound} \end{aligned}$$ The limit in Equation <a href="#zero:GRR-base" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="zero:GRR-base">[zero:GRR-base]</a> is a limit of real Bott–Chern classes.
  - Explanation: Direct images have asymptotic rank given by the fiber volume, and their determinant limit yields a volume bound for the total space.
- **claim-slope-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-slope-descent)): Let $Y$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold and $\mathscr E$ a holomorphic vector bundle of rank at least two. Write $\pi_{\mathscr E}:\mathbb{P}_Y(\mathscr E)\to Y$ and $\zeta_{\mathscr E}=c_1(\mathcal O_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr E)}(1))$. Suppose that a real class $D$ on $Y$ satisfies $$c_1(\mathscr H)\le kD \quad\text{whenever}\quad 0\ne\bigl(\mathscr H\longrightarrow\mathscr E^{\otimes k}\bigr)$$ for a line bundle $\mathscr H$ and integer $k\ge0$. For a real class $A$ on $Y$ and a real number $b\ge0$, $$\label{zero:slope-descent-equation} \pi_{\mathscr E}^*A+b\zeta_{\mathscr E}\ge0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad A+bD\ge0 .$$
  - Explanation: Pseudo-effectivity on a projective bundle descends to the base after adding the slope bound class.
- **claim-cotangent-bundle-z** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-cotangent-z)): Let $\mathscr H$ be a holomorphic line bundle on $Z$, and suppose there is a nonzero map $\mathscr H\to\Omega_Z^{\otimes k}$, with $k\ge0$. Then $$\mathscr H=\pi^*(\mathscr Q_1\boxtimes\mathscr Q_2) \otimes\mathcal O_Z(\ell)$$ for lines $\mathscr Q_i$ on $X$ and an integer $\ell$, and $$\ell\le0,\qquad c_1(\mathscr Q_1)_1+c_1(\mathscr Q_2)_2 \le(k-\ell)(L_1+L_2).$$
  - Explanation: A line mapping into a cotangent tensor on the projective bundle has nonpositive vertical degree and bounded horizontal classes.
- **claim-diagonal-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-diagonal-mass)): There are constants $c_n,C_n>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large $q$, one can choose a rational number $$c_nA_q\le s\le C_nA_q$$ and a Kähler current $T$ in $C_s$ with analytic singularities, not generically singular on $E$, with the following property. On a smooth projective modification $h:U\to E$, its restricted mass has a rational-divisor approximation $$\label{zero:selected-decomposition} h^*(2M+s\zeta)=\beta+\{D'\}, \qquad \beta\ \text{Kähler},\quad D'\ge0\ \text{rational},$$ which retains all log-ideal orders of $T\|_E$. If $f:U\to Z$ is the natural map and $$w=\int_{U_z}\beta^{d-1}$$ on a general fiber, then $$\label{zero:selected-w} w\ge c_ns^{d-1}.$$
  - Explanation: One can choose a comparable scale and a Kähler current whose restricted mass gives a definite fiber volume on the diagonal exceptional divisor.
- **claim-incidence-pole** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-incidence-pole)): The pole just defined satisfies $$\label{zero:pole-transfer} b_V\ge s-C_n .$$
  - Explanation: The selected current has a large pole along the incidence submanifold, bounded below by the scale minus a constant.
- **claim-determinant-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-determinant-order)): On a smooth projective modification $h_+:U^+\to E^+$ one can choose an approximation $$\label{zero:plus-decomposition} h_+^*(2p_+^*M+s\zeta)=\beta^++\{D^+\}, \qquad \beta^+\ \text{Kähler},\quad D^+\ge0\ \text{rational},$$ retaining the log-ideal orders of $T\|_E$, such that the general fiber volume $w^+$ of $\beta^+$ over $Z^+$ satisfies $w^+\ge w/2$. Let $f_+:U^+\to Z^+$ be the natural map and put $$\Lambda^+=s h_+^*\mathcal O_{E^+}(1)-\mathcal O_{U^+}(D^+),\qquad \mathcal F_j^+=(f_+)_*\mathcal O_{U^+}(j\Lambda^+),\qquad R_j^+=\operatorname{rk}\mathcal F_j^+ .$$ For every sufficiently large divisible $j$, write $$\iota_j:\mathcal F_j^+\hookrightarrow p_+^*\operatorname{Sym}^{js}\Omega_Z,\qquad \varphi_j:\det\mathcal F_j^+\longrightarrow \bigwedge^{R_j^+}\!\bigl(p_+^*\operatorname{Sym}^{js}\Omega_Z\bigr)$$ for the inclusion and its nonzero determinant map. Here $\det\mathcal F_j^+=(\bigwedge^{R_j^+}\mathcal F_j^+)^{**}$, and the determinant map extends across the complement of the locally free locus, which has codimension at least two. Localize a coordinate local ring of $Z^+$ at the height-one prime of $J_+$, obtaining a discrete valuation ring $R$ with uniformizer $u$. In local frames over $R$, define $$h_j=\min\{\operatorname{ord}_u(c):c\text{ is a nonzero coefficient of }\varphi_j\}.$$ Equivalently, $h_j$ is the minimum order of the maximal minors of $\iota_j$; it is independent of the frames. If $\sigma_{J_+}$ is the canonical section of $\mathcal O_{Z^+}(J_+)$, then $\varphi_j$ factors as $$\det\mathcal F_j^+ \xrightarrow{\ \cdot\sigma_{J_+}^{h_j}\ } \det\mathcal F_j^+(h_jJ_+) \xrightarrow{\ \widetilde\varphi_j\ } \bigwedge^{R_j^+}\!\bigl(p_+^*\operatorname{Sym}^{js}\Omega_Z\bigr),$$ where $\widetilde\varphi_j$ is holomorphic. The common order satisfies $$\label{zero:determinant-order-equation} h_j\ge c_n\,jR_j^+s ,$$ provided $q$ is sufficiently large.
  - Explanation: The incidence pole forces a large common vanishing order of the determinant map along the exceptional divisor.
- **claim-simple-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-zero-simple-vanishing)): Let $X$ be a smooth simple compact Kähler manifold of algebraic dimension zero carrying a generically nondegenerate holomorphic two-form. Every nef rational holomorphic line bundle on $X$ has zero first Chern class.
  - Explanation: On simple symplectic Kähler manifolds of algebraic dimension zero, every nef rational line has zero class.
- **claim-cycle-sections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cycle-sections)): Let $h:X\to W$ be a holomorphic algebraic reduction of a smooth connected compact complex manifold, with connected fibers and smooth projective base. Let $(L_i)_{i\in I}$ be a countable collection of rational holomorphic line bundles on $X$. Outside a countable union of nowhere dense subsets of $W$, a smooth fiber $F$ of $h$ satisfies $$\kappa(F,L_i\|_F)\leq 0 \qquad\text{for every }i\in I.$$ Any prescribed countable collection of further conditions holding on dense open subsets of $W$ can be imposed on the same fiber.
  - Explanation: For a countable family of lines, a general fiber of the algebraic reduction has nonpositive Iitaka dimension for each, by coherent base change and Baire's theorem.
- **claim-cycle-divisors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cycle-divisors)): Let $F$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let $A$ be a rational line bundle on $F$, and suppose that a modification $\rho:\widehat F\to F$ from a smooth compact Kähler manifold satisfies $$\label{cycle:fiber-decomposition} \rho^*A\equiv P+R, \qquad P\text{ semiample}, \qquad R=N(c_1(\rho^*A))\text{ a rational divisor}.$$ Let $g:\widehat F\to Q$ be the fibration defined by $P$, so that $Q$ is normal projective and $P\sim_{\mathbb Q}g^*H_Q$ for an ample rational line bundle $H_Q$. There is a dense open subset $U\subset F$, independent of the divisors below, with the following properties for every sufficiently divisible positive integer $m$.* 1. *There exists an analytic family of effective divisors on $F$ with class $m c_1(A)$ that distinguishes the general fibers of the meromorphic fibration $g\circ\rho^{-1}$.* 2. *For every effective divisor $D$ with class $m c_1(A)$, membership in $\operatorname{Supp}D$ is constant along the intersection of $U$ with a general smooth fiber of that meromorphic fibration.
  - Explanation: Divisors in a multiple of the class distinguish the general fibers of the semiample fibration, and their supports are constant along those fibers.
- **claim-cycle-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cycle-detection)): Let $h:X\to W$ be a fibration of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds with $\dim W<\dim X$, and let $A$ be a rational line bundle on $X$. There is a countable collection of diagrams $$\label{cycle:diagrams} \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center),>=Stealth] \node (Xi) at (0,1.15) {$X_i$}; \node (Zi) at (2.7,1.15) {$Z_i$}; \node (X) at (0,0) {$X$}; \node (W) at (2.7,0) {$W$}; \draw[->] (Xi) -- node[above] {$f_i$} (Zi); \draw[->] (Xi) -- node[left] {$p_i$} (X); \draw[->] (Zi) -- node[right] {$k_i$} (W); \draw[->] (X) -- node[below] {$h$} (W); \end{tikzpicture}$$ where $p_i$ is a modification, $f_i$ is a fibration, and $X_i,Z_i$ are smooth compact Kähler manifolds, with the following property.* *For a very general smooth fiber $F=X_w$, suppose that a smooth compact Kähler modification $\rho:\widehat F\to F$ has a decomposition <a href="#cycle:fiber-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cycle:fiber-decomposition">[cycle:fiber-decomposition]</a> for $A\|_F$, and that the fibration $g:\widehat F\to Q$ defined by $P$ has positive-dimensional base. For some $i$, the restriction of $f_i$ over $w$ is bimeromorphically equivalent to $g$. In particular, on a common resolution, their general fibers coincide. The collection and the exceptional subsets of $W$ can be fixed before $F$, $\rho$, $P$, and $R$ are chosen.
  - Explanation: A countable collection of global fibrations detects the fiberwise semiample fibration on very general fibers.
- **claim-cycle-nef-summand** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cycle-nef-summand)): Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:decomposition">2</a> in dimensions less than $n$. Let $T$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler $n$-fold with $0<a(T)<n$, and let $h:T\to W$ be a holomorphic algebraic reduction with smooth projective base and connected fibers. Let $B$ be an effective rational SNC divisor with coefficients less than one, assume that $J=K_T+B$ is pseudo-effective, and let $M$ be a nef rational line bundle. Then $$c_1(M\|_F)=0$$ for a general smooth fiber $F$ of $h$.
  - Explanation: With intermediate algebraic dimension, the nef summand has zero class on a general fiber of the algebraic reduction.
- **claim-cycle-base-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cycle-base-descent)): Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href="#cycle:nef-summand" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cycle:nef-summand">36</a>, there are modifications $p:T'\to T$ and $b:W'\to W$, with $T'$ smooth compact Kähler and $W'$ smooth projective, a fibration $h':T'\to W'$ lifting $h$, and a nef rational line bundle $N$ on $W'$ such that $$p^*M\equiv(h')^*N.$$
  - Explanation: Fiberwise vanishing lets the nef summand descend numerically to a nef rational line on a projective base after modifications.
- **claim-exceptional-negative-part** (theorem, [source](#stmt-finish-exceptional)): Let $r:V\to S$ be a surjective morphism from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a normal projective variety. Let $H$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor on $S$. If $E\geq0$ is a real divisor on $V$ whose every component has image of codimension at least two in $S$, then $$N(r^*H+E)=E.$$
  - Explanation: A divisor mapping to codimension at least two on top of a nef pullback is exactly the negative part, proved via mixed Hodge–Riemann relations.
- **claim-program-over-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-finish-over-base)): Let $T$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $n$, let $(T,B)$ be a rational simple normal crossing klt pair with $J=K_T+B$ pseudo-effective, and let $h:T\to W$ be a surjective morphism to a smooth projective variety. There are a smooth compact Kähler modification $T_1\to T$, an effective klt adjoint $J_1$ on $T_1$ as in <a href="#pre:resolution-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:resolution-error">[pre:resolution-error]</a>, and a finite ordinary $J_1$-negative program over $W$, ending at a normal compact Kähler klt pair $(Y,\Delta)$, such that for some positive integer $b$ and an ample Cartier divisor $A_W$ on $W$, $$K_Y+\Delta+b h_Y^*A_W$$ is semiample. Here $h_Y:Y\to W$ is the descended morphism.
  - Explanation: A negative program over the algebraic base, with scaling by a multiple of an ample line from the base, ends on a klt model where the augmented adjoint is semiample, preserving the map to the base.

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-decomposition",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, $B\\geq0$ a rational simple normal crossing divisor with coefficients less than one, and $M$ a nef rational line bundle.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pre-picard",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu:Y\\to X$ be a modification between smooth compact Kähler manifolds.*\n\n1.  *A rational line whose real Chern class is zero belongs to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X)\\otimes_{\\mathbb Z}\\mathbb Q$.*\n\n2.  *Pullback identifies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X)$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(Y)$.*\n\n3.  *If a rational line $L$ on $X$ has semiample pullback, then $L$ is semiample.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-pre-picard",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pre-negative",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\alpha$ be pseudo-effective on a smooth compact Kähler manifold.*\n\n1.  *Every positive current in $\\alpha$ contains $[N(\\alpha)]$. If $0\\leq F\\leq N(\\alpha)$, then $$N(\\alpha-\\{F\\})=N(\\alpha)-F.$$*\n\n2.  *If $\\beta$ is nef, then $N(\\alpha+\\beta)\\leq N(\\alpha)$. In particular $N(\\beta)=0$.*\n\n3.  *A modified nef class restricts pseudo-effectively to a resolution of every prime divisor.*\n\n4.  *If $\\mu:Y\\to X$ is a smooth modification and $\\alpha=\\beta+\\{R\\}$, with $\\beta$ nef and $R=N(\\alpha)$, then $$N(\\mu^*\\alpha)=\\mu^*R.$$*\n\n5.  *Suppose $\\mu:Y\\to X$ is a modification from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a normal compact Kähler space, $A$ is a rational line on $X$, and $E\\geq0$ is $\\mu$-exceptional. If $\\mu^*A+E$ is pseudo-effective, then $\\mu^*A$ is pseudo-effective and $$N(\\mu^*A+E)=N(\\mu^*A)+E.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-pre-negative",

In particular $N(\\beta)=0$.*\n\n3.  *A modified nef class restricts pseudo-effectively to a resolution of every prime divisor.*\n\n4.  *If $\\mu:Y\\to X$ is a smooth modification and $\\alpha=\\beta+\\{R\\}$, with $\\beta$ nef and $R=N(\\alpha)$, then $$N(\\mu^*\\alpha)=\\mu^*R.$$*\n\n5.  *Suppose $\\mu:Y\\to X$ is a modification from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a normal compact Kähler space, $A$ is a rational line on $X$, and $E\\geq0$ is $\\mu$-exceptional.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pre-contract",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,B)$ be a smooth compact Kähler klt pair with rational simple normal crossing boundary. Suppose $$K_X+B\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}P+R,\\qquad P\\text{ semiample},\\qquad\n R=N(K_X+B)\\geq0$$ with $P,R$ rational. A finite ordinary $(K_X+B)$-negative program reaches a normal compact Kähler klt model on which the log canonical line is semiample. Its steps contract or flip detected analytic extremal rays, are $P$-trivial, and preserve a fixed generated Cartier multiple of $P$ as an actual line. The final adjoint is rationally linearly equivalent to the descended semiample line. The steps extract no divisors; on a common resolution the initial adjoint equals the pullback of the final adjoint plus an effective divisor exceptional over the final model.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-pre-projective",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(S,\\Delta)$ be a normal projective klt pair with rational boundary, and let $M$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor. Suppose $K_S+\\Delta$ is pseudo-effective. There is a smooth projective modification $u:S'\\to S$, a birational morphism $v:S'\\to S_m$ to a normal projective variety, a nef rational Cartier divisor $H_m$ on $S_m$, and an effective rational $v$-exceptional divisor $E$, such that $$\\label{pre:projective-model}\n u^*(K_S+\\Delta+M)\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}v^*H_m+E.$$ On a sufficiently high such model, $v^*H_m$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample rational divisor and $$N\\bigl(u^*(K_S+\\Delta+M)\\bigr)=E.$$",

        "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Y$ be a fibration of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, and let $M\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\\otimes\\mathbb Q$ be analytically nef. There are smooth compact Kähler modifications $p:X'\\to X$ and $q:Y'\\to Y$, a fibration $g:X'\\to Y'$ satisfying $fp=qg$, and a rational line $N$ on $Y'$ such that $$\\label{desc:numerical-identity}\n                 p^*M\\equiv g^*N.$$ If $Y$ is projective, $Y'$ can be chosen projective and $N$ is nef.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-adj-base",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(Y,D)$ be an effective klt pair with rational boundary on a normal compact Kähler space. Let $f:Y\\to Z$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers to a normal projective variety, and suppose that $\\dim Y>\\dim Z$. If $H$ is a rational Cartier line bundle on $Z$ and $$\\label{adj:initial}\n                         K_Y+D\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}f^*H,$$ then there is an effective rational divisor $D_Z$ such that $(Z,D_Z)$ is klt and $$H\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}K_Z+D_Z.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-adj-period-input",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a smooth projective variety and let $S^\\circ\\subset S$ have simple normal crossing complement. Let $\\mathbb V$ be a real-polarizable pure variation on $S^\\circ$ with an integral lattice and quasi-unipotent local monodromy. Suppose that a complex direct summand of $\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C}$, as a variation of Hodge structures, has a rank-one highest Hodge piece, with parabolic extension $L$. Then $L$ is nef, and its numerical dimension is the generic rank of its line period map.*\n\n\n*After modification $\\tau:S'\\to S$, there are a smooth projective variety $Q$, a surjective morphism $p:S'\\to Q$ with connected fibers, and a nef rational line bundle $P$ on $Q$ such that $$\\label{adj:period-descent}\n                         \\tau^*L\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}p^*P.$$ Moreover, $\\dim Q$ is at most the generic rank of the full period map of $\\mathbb V$. If $Q$ is a point, $\\tau^*L$ is rationally trivial.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Suppose that a complex direct summand of $\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb C}$, as a variation of Hodge structures, has a rank-one highest Hodge piece, with parabolic extension $L$. Then $L$ is nef, and its numerical dimension is the generic rank of its line period map.*\n\n\n*After modification $\\tau:S'\\to S$, there are a smooth projective variety $Q$, a surjective morphism $p:S'\\to Q$ with connected fibers, and a nef rational line bundle $P$ on $Q$ such that $$\\label{adj:period-descent}\n                         \\tau^*L\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}p^*P.$$ Moreover, $\\dim Q$ is at most the generic rank of the full period map of $\\mathbb V$.         "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth simple compact Kähler manifold of dimension $n\\ge2$, algebraic dimension zero and irregularity zero, and suppose $K_X$ is pseudo-effective. Let $\\mathscr L$ be a holomorphic line bundle such that $L=c_1(\\mathscr L)\\ge c_1(K_X)$ in pseudo-effective order. For every nonzero map $\\mathscr H\\to\\Omega_X^{\\otimes k}$, where $\\mathscr H$ is a line bundle and $k\\ge0$, $$\\label{zero:slope-X}\n c_1(\\mathscr H)\\le kL.$$ For every very general $x\\in X$, write $a:Y=\\operatorname{Bl}_xX\\to X$. Every nonzero line map $\\mathscr H_Y\\to(a^*\\Omega_X)^{\\otimes k}$ satisfies $$\\label{zero:slope-blowup}\n c_1(\\mathscr H_Y)\\le ka^*L.$$ The exceptional set of points can be chosen simultaneously for all $\\mathscr H_Y$ and $k$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-zero-line-nonvanishing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth simple compact Kähler manifold of dimension $n\\ge2$, with $a(X)=0$ and irregularity zero. Assume that the only positive-dimensional proper compact irreducible analytic subvarieties through a very general point of $X^2$ are its factor slices. Let $\\mathscr L$ be a holomorphic line bundle with pseudo-effective class $L$. Assume the following line-subsheaf bounds:*\n\n1.  *For every line bundle $\\mathscr H$, every integer $k\\ge0$, and every nonzero map $\\mathscr H\\to\\Omega_X^{\\otimes k}$, one has $c_1(\\mathscr H)\\le kL$.*\n\n2.  *Outside a countable union of proper analytic subsets of $X$, every point $x$ has this property: if $a:Y=\\operatorname{Bl}_xX\\to X$, then for every line bundle $\\mathscr H_Y$, every $k\\ge0$, and every nonzero map $\\mathscr H_Y\\to(a^*\\Omega_X)^{\\otimes k}$, one has $c_1(\\mathscr H_Y)\\le ka^*L$.*\n\n*Then some positive power of $\\mathscr L$ has a nonzero meromorphic section.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-zero-pole-threshold",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $e\\ge2$, $\\alpha$ a big real $(1,1)$-class, and $z\\in W$. Suppose a smooth projective modification $\\mu:W'\\to W$, which is an isomorphism near $z$, admits a decomposition $$\\mu^*\\alpha=K+\\{D\\},\\qquad K\\ \\text{Kähler},\\quad D\\ge0,$$ where $D$ misses the point $z'$ over $z$. If the ordinary analytic Seshadri constant $\\epsilon(K,z')$ is at least $\\eta>0$, then, on the blowup $b:\\widehat W=\\operatorname{Bl}_zW\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $G$, $$\\label{zero:pole-formula}\n \\sup\\{u\\ge0:b^*\\alpha-u\\{G\\}\\ge0\\}\n \\le \\frac{\\eta}{2}\n     +2^{e-1}\\operatorname{vol}(\\alpha)\\eta^{-(e-1)} .$$",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-zero-pole-threshold",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $e\\ge2$, $\\alpha$ a big real $(1,1)$-class, and $z\\in W$. Suppose a smooth projective modification $\\mu:W'\\to W$, which is an isomorphism near $z$, admits a decomposition $$\\mu^*\\alpha=K+\\{D\\},\\qquad K\\ \\text{Kähler},\\quad D\\ge0,$$ where $D$ misses the point $z'$ over $z$. If the ordinary analytic Seshadri constant $\\epsilon(K,z')$ is at least $\\eta>0$, then, on the blowup $b:\\widehat W=\\operatorname{Bl}_zW\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $G$, $$\\label{zero:pole-formula}\n \\sup\\{u\\ge0:b^*\\alpha-u\\{G\\}\\ge0\\}\n \\le \\frac{\\eta}{2}\n     +2^{e-1}\\operatorname{vol}(\\alpha)\\eta^{-(e-1)} .$$",
          "source_id": "PM-036.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $W$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $e\\ge2$, $\\alpha$ a big real $(1,1)$-class, and $z\\in W$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-zero-nef-pushforward",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:U\\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\\dim Z_0=b_0\\ge1$ and $\\dim U=b_0+e$, where $e\\ge1$. Let $\\beta$ be a Kähler class, $w=\\int_{U_z}\\beta^e$ its general fiber volume, and $$B_0=\\frac{f_*\\beta^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}.$$ There are smooth projective modifications $p:Z'_0\\to Z_0$ and $q:U'\\to U$, and a morphism $f':U'\\to Z'_0$ with $pf'=fq$, such that $$B'_0:=\\frac{f'_*(q^*\\beta)^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}\n       =p^*B_0-\\{D_0\\}$$ is nef and $D_0$ is an effective $p$-exceptional real divisor.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-zero-nef-pushforward",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:U\\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\\dim Z_0=b_0\\ge1$ and $\\dim U=b_0+e$, where $e\\ge1$. Let $\\beta$ be a Kähler class, $w=\\int_{U_z}\\beta^e$ its general fiber volume, and $$B_0=\\frac{f_*\\beta^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}.$$ There are smooth projective modifications $p:Z'_0\\to Z_0$ and $q:U'\\to U$, and a morphism $f':U'\\to Z'_0$ with $pf'=fq$, such that $$B'_0:=\\frac{f'_*(q^*\\beta)^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}\n       =p^*B_0-\\{D_0\\}$$ is nef and $D_0$ is an effective $p$-exceptional real divisor.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $f:U\\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\\dim Z_0=b_0\\ge1$ and $\\dim U=b_0+e$, where $e\\ge1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-zero-direct-image",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:U\\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\\dim Z_0=b_0\\ge1$ and $\\dim U=b_0+e$, $e\\ge1$. Suppose $$\\beta=f^*G+c_1(\\Lambda)$$ is a Kähler class, where $G\\in H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(Z_0,\\mathbb R)$ and $\\Lambda\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(U)\\otimes\\mathbb Q$. Put $$w=\\int_{U_z}\\beta^e>0$$ on a general fiber. For sufficiently large divisible integers $j$, let $\\mathcal F_j=f_*\\mathcal O_U(j\\Lambda)$ and $R_j=\\operatorname{rk}\\mathcal F_j$. Here $j\\Lambda$ is an actual line bundle and $c_1(\\mathcal F_j)$ means $c_1(\\det\\mathcal F_j)$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n R_j&=\\frac{w}{e!}j^e+O(j^{e-1}),\\label{zero:GRR-rank}\\\\\n B_0:=G+\\lim_j\\frac{c_1(\\mathcal F_j)}{jR_j}\n &=\\frac{f_*\\beta^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}\\ge0,\\label{zero:GRR-base}\\\\\n \\int_U\\beta^{b_0+e}\n &\\le (e+1)^{b_0}w\\,\\operatorname{vol}(B_0).\\label{zero:mixed-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The limit in Equation <a href=\"#zero:GRR-base\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"zero:GRR-base\">[zero:GRR-base]</a> is a limit of real Bott–Chern classes.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-zero-direct-image",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:U\\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\\dim Z_0=b_0\\ge1$ and $\\dim U=b_0+e$, $e\\ge1$. Suppose $$\\beta=f^*G+c_1(\\Lambda)$$ is a Kähler class, where $G\\in H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(Z_0,\\mathbb R)$ and $\\Lambda\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(U)\\otimes\\mathbb Q$. Put $$w=\\int_{U_z}\\beta^e>0$$ on a general fiber. For sufficiently large divisible integers $j$, let $\\mathcal F_j=f_*\\mathcal O_U(j\\Lambda)$ and $R_j=\\operatorname{rk}\\mathcal F_j$. Here $j\\Lambda$ is an actual line bundle and $c_1(\\mathcal F_j)$ means $c_1(\\det\\mathcal F_j)$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n R_j&=\\frac{w}{e!}j^e+O(j^{e-1}),\\label{zero:GRR-rank}\\\\\n B_0:=G+\\lim_j\\frac{c_1(\\mathcal F_j)}{jR_j}\n &=\\frac{f_*\\beta^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}\\ge0,\\label{zero:GRR-base}\\\\\n \\int_U\\beta^{b_0+e}\n &\\le (e+1)^{b_0}w\\,\\operatorname{vol}(B_0).\\label{zero:mixed-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The limit in Equation <a href=\"#zero:GRR-base\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"zero:GRR-base\">[zero:GRR-base]</a> is a limit of real Bott–Chern classes.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $f:U\\to Z_0$ be a surjective projective morphism of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds, with $\\dim Z_0=b_0\\ge1$ and $\\dim U=b_0+e$, $e\\ge1$. Suppose $$\\beta=f^*G+c_1(\\Lambda)$$ is a Kähler class, where $G\\in H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(Z_0,\\mathbb R)$ and $\\Lambda\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(U)\\otimes\\mathbb Q$. Put $$w=\\int_{U_z}\\beta^e>0$$ on a general fiber. For sufficiently large divisible integers $j$, let $\\mathcal F_j=f_*\\mathcal O_U(j\\Lambda)$ and $R_j=\\operatorname{rk}\\mathcal F_j$. Here $j\\Lambda$ is an actual line bundle and $c_1(\\mathcal F_j)$ means $c_1(\\det\\mathcal F_j)$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n R_j&=\\frac{w}{e!}j^e+O(j^{e-1}),\\label{zero:GRR-rank}\\\\\n B_0:=G+\\lim_j\\frac{c_1(\\mathcal F_j)}{jR_j}\n &=\\frac{f_*\\beta^{e+1}}{(e+1)w}\\ge0,\\label{zero:GRR-base}\\\\\n \\int_U\\beta^{b_0+e}\n &\\le (e+1)^{b_0}w\\,\\operatorname{vol}(B_0).\\label{zero:mixed-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The limit in Equation <a href=\"#zero:GRR-base\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"zero:GRR-base\">[zero:GRR-base]</a> is a limit of real Bott–Chern classes.",
      "explanation": "Direct images have asymptotic rank given by the fiber volume, and their determinant limit yields a volume bound for the total space.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-zero-slope-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold and $\\mathscr E$ a holomorphic vector bundle of rank at least two. Write $\\pi_{\\mathscr E}:\\mathbb{P}_Y(\\mathscr E)\\to Y$ and $\\zeta_{\\mathscr E}=c_1(\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb{P}(\\mathscr E)}(1))$. Suppose that a real class $D$ on $Y$ satisfies $$c_1(\\mathscr H)\\le kD\n \\quad\\text{whenever}\\quad\n 0\\ne\\bigl(\\mathscr H\\longrightarrow\\mathscr E^{\\otimes k}\\bigr)$$ for a line bundle $\\mathscr H$ and integer $k\\ge0$. For a real class $A$ on $Y$ and a real number $b\\ge0$, $$\\label{zero:slope-descent-equation}\n \\pi_{\\mathscr E}^*A+b\\zeta_{\\mathscr E}\\ge0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad A+bD\\ge0 .$$",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For a real class $A$ on $Y$ and a real number $b\\ge0$, $$\\label{zero:slope-descent-equation}\n \\pi_{\\mathscr E}^*A+b\\zeta_{\\mathscr E}\\ge0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad A+bD\\ge0 .$$",
          "source_id": "PM-036.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-zero-diagonal-mass",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_n,C_n>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large $q$, one can choose a rational number $$c_nA_q\\le s\\le C_nA_q$$ and a Kähler current $T$ in $C_s$ with analytic singularities, not generically singular on $E$, with the following property. On a smooth projective modification $h:U\\to E$, its restricted mass has a rational-divisor approximation $$\\label{zero:selected-decomposition}\n h^*(2M+s\\zeta)=\\beta+\\{D'\\},\n \\qquad \\beta\\ \\text{Kähler},\\quad D'\\ge0\\ \\text{rational},$$ which retains all log-ideal orders of $T|_E$.         "source_id": "PM-036.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-zero-determinant-order",
        "exact_quote": "On a smooth projective modification $h_+:U^+\\to E^+$ one can choose an approximation $$\\label{zero:plus-decomposition}\n h_+^*(2p_+^*M+s\\zeta)=\\beta^++\\{D^+\\},\n \\qquad \\beta^+\\ \\text{Kähler},\\quad D^+\\ge0\\ \\text{rational},$$ retaining the log-ideal orders of $T|_E$, such that the general fiber volume $w^+$ of $\\beta^+$ over $Z^+$ satisfies $w^+\\ge w/2$. Let $f_+:U^+\\to Z^+$ be the natural map and put $$\\Lambda^+=s h_+^*\\mathcal O_{E^+}(1)-\\mathcal O_{U^+}(D^+),\\qquad\n \\mathcal F_j^+=(f_+)_*\\mathcal O_{U^+}(j\\Lambda^+),\\qquad\n R_j^+=\\operatorname{rk}\\mathcal F_j^+ .$$ For every sufficiently large divisible $j$, write $$\\iota_j:\\mathcal F_j^+\\hookrightarrow p_+^*\\operatorname{Sym}^{js}\\Omega_Z,\\qquad\n \\varphi_j:\\det\\mathcal F_j^+\\longrightarrow\n       \\bigwedge^{R_j^+}\\!\\bigl(p_+^*\\operatorname{Sym}^{js}\\Omega_Z\\bigr)$$ for the inclusion and its nonzero determinant map. Here $\\det\\mathcal F_j^+=(\\bigwedge^{R_j^+}\\mathcal F_j^+)^{**}$, and the determinant map extends across the complement of the locally free locus, which has codimension at least two. Localize a coordinate local ring of $Z^+$ at the height-one prime of $J_+$, obtaining a discrete valuation ring $R$ with uniformizer $u$. In local frames over $R$, define $$h_j=\\min\\{\\operatorname{ord}_u(c):c\\text{ is a nonzero coefficient of }\\varphi_j\\}.$$ Equivalently, $h_j$ is the minimum order of the maximal minors of $\\iota_j$; it is independent of the frames. If $\\sigma_{J_+}$ is the canonical section of $\\mathcal O_{Z^+}(J_+)$, then $\\varphi_j$ factors as $$\\det\\mathcal F_j^+\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\cdot\\sigma_{J_+}^{h_j}\\ }\n \\det\\mathcal F_j^+(h_jJ_+)\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\widetilde\\varphi_j\\ }\n \\bigwedge^{R_j^+}\\!\\bigl(p_+^*\\operatorname{Sym}^{js}\\Omega_Z\\bigr),$$ where $\\widetilde\\varphi_j$ is holomorphic. The common order satisfies $$\\label{zero:determinant-order-equation}\n h_j\\ge c_n\\,jR_j^+s ,$$ provided $q$ is sufficiently large.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cycle-sections",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:X\\to W$ be a holomorphic algebraic reduction of a smooth connected compact complex manifold, with connected fibers and smooth projective base. Let $(L_i)_{i\\in I}$ be a countable collection of rational holomorphic line bundles on $X$. Outside a countable union of nowhere dense subsets of $W$, a smooth fiber $F$ of $h$ satisfies $$\\kappa(F,L_i|_F)\\leq 0\n                         \\qquad\\text{for every }i\\in I.$$ Any prescribed countable collection of further conditions holding on dense open subsets of $W$ can be imposed on the same fiber.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cycle-sections",

          "source_id": "PM-036.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $h:X\\to W$ be a holomorphic algebraic reduction of a smooth connected compact complex manifold, with connected fibers and smooth projective base.       "explanation": "For a countable family of lines, a general fiber of the algebraic reduction has nonpositive Iitaka dimension for each, by coherent base change and Baire's theorem.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cycle-divisors",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let $A$ be a rational line bundle on $F$, and suppose that a modification $\\rho:\\widehat F\\to F$ from a smooth compact Kähler manifold satisfies $$\\label{cycle:fiber-decomposition}\n \\rho^*A\\equiv P+R,\n \\qquad P\\text{ semiample},\n \\qquad R=N(c_1(\\rho^*A))\\text{ a rational divisor}.$$ Let $g:\\widehat F\\to Q$ be the fibration defined by $P$, so that $Q$ is normal projective and $P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}g^*H_Q$ for an ample rational line bundle $H_Q$. There is a dense open subset $U\\subset F$, independent of the divisors below, with the following properties for every sufficiently divisible positive integer $m$.*\n\n1.  *There exists an analytic family of effective divisors on $F$ with class $m c_1(A)$ that distinguishes the general fibers of the meromorphic fibration $g\\circ\\rho^{-1}$.*\n\n2.  *For every effective divisor $D$ with class $m c_1(A)$, membership in $\\operatorname{Supp}D$ is constant along the intersection of $U$ with a general smooth fiber of that meromorphic fibration.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cycle-divisors",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let $A$ be a rational line bundle on $F$, and suppose that a modification $\\rho:\\widehat F\\to F$ from a smooth compact Kähler manifold satisfies $$\\label{cycle:fiber-decomposition}\n \\rho^*A\\equiv P+R,\n \\qquad P\\text{ semiample},\n \\qquad R=N(c_1(\\rho^*A))\\text{ a rational divisor}.$$ Let $g:\\widehat F\\to Q$ be the fibration defined by $P$, so that $Q$ is normal projective and $P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}g^*H_Q$ for an ample rational line bundle $H_Q$. There is a dense open subset $U\\subset F$, independent of the divisors below, with the following properties for every sufficiently divisible positive integer $m$.*\n\n1.  *There exists an analytic family of effective divisors on $F$ with class $m c_1(A)$ that distinguishes the general fibers of the meromorphic fibration $g\\circ\\rho^{-1}$.*\n\n2.  *For every effective divisor $D$ with class $m c_1(A)$, membership in $\\operatorname{Supp}D$ is constant along the intersection of $U$ with a general smooth fiber of that meromorphic fibration.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $F$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let $A$ be a rational line bundle on $F$, and suppose that a modification $\\rho:\\widehat F\\to F$ from a smooth compact Kähler manifold satisfies $$\\label{cycle:fiber-decomposition}\n \\rho^*A\\equiv P+R,\n \\qquad P\\text{ semiample},\n \\qquad R=N(c_1(\\rho^*A))\\text{ a rational divisor}.$$ Let $g:\\widehat F\\to Q$ be the fibration defined by $P$, so that $Q$ is normal projective and $P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}g^*H_Q$ for an ample rational line bundle $H_Q$. There is a dense open subset $U\\subset F$, independent of the divisors below, with the following properties for every sufficiently divisible positive integer $m$.*\n\n1.  *There exists an analytic family of effective divisors on $F$ with class $m c_1(A)$ that distinguishes the general fibers of the meromorphic fibration $g\\circ\\rho^{-1}$.*\n\n2.  *For every effective divisor $D$ with class $m c_1(A)$, membership in $\\operatorname{Supp}D$ is constant along the intersection of $U$ with a general smooth fiber of that meromorphic fibration.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cycle-detection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:X\\to W$ be a fibration of smooth connected compact Kähler manifolds with $\\dim W<\\dim X$, and let $A$ be a rational line bundle on $X$. There is a countable collection of diagrams $$\\label{cycle:diagrams}\n \\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center),>=Stealth]\n \\node (Xi) at (0,1.15) {$X_i$};\n \\node (Zi) at (2.7,1.15) {$Z_i$};\n \\node (X) at (0,0) {$X$};\n \\node (W) at (2.7,0) {$W$};\n \\draw[->] (Xi) -- node[above] {$f_i$} (Zi);\n \\draw[->] (Xi) -- node[left] {$p_i$} (X);\n \\draw[->] (Zi) -- node[right] {$k_i$} (W);\n \\draw[->] (X) -- node[below] {$h$} (W);\n \\end{tikzpicture}$$ where $p_i$ is a modification, $f_i$ is a fibration, and $X_i,Z_i$ are smooth compact Kähler manifolds, with the following property.*\n\n*For a very general smooth fiber $F=X_w$, suppose that a smooth compact Kähler modification $\\rho:\\widehat F\\to F$ has a decomposition <a href=\"#cycle:fiber-decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cycle:fiber-decomposition\">[cycle:fiber-decomposition]</a> for $A|_F$, and that the fibration $g:\\widehat F\\to Q$ defined by $P$ has positive-dimensional base. For some $i$, the restriction of $f_i$ over $w$ is bimeromorphically equivalent to $g$. In particular, on a common resolution, their general fibers coincide. The collection and the exceptional subsets of $W$ can be fixed before $F$, $\\rho$, $P$, and $R$ are chosen.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **rational line bundle** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A rational line bundle on a complex space $X$ means an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$; we use additive notation.
- **numerical equivalence** ([source](#pm-h-6)): On a smooth compact Kähler manifold, $L\equiv L'$ means $c_1(L)=c_1(L')$ in real Bott–Chern cohomology.
- **pseudo-effective and nef classes** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A class is *pseudo-effective* if it contains a closed positive current; it is *nef* if it lies in the closure of the Kähler cone.
- **semiample rational line** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A rational line is *semiample* if a positive integral multiple is generated by its global sections.
- **modification** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A modification is a proper bimeromorphic morphism.
- **sub-klt pair** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A signed pair with all discrepancies greater than $-1$ is called sub-klt.
- **minimal multiplicity** ([source](#pm-h-7)): If $E$ is a prime divisor, its minimal multiplicity is $$\nu_E(\alpha)=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \inf\{\nu_E(T):T\in\alpha,\quad T\geq-\varepsilon\omega\}.$$
- **fibration** ([source](#sec:descent)): Throughout this section a *fibration* is a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers.
- **algebraic dimension** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The algebraic dimension $a(T)$ is the transcendence degree of the field of meromorphic functions on $T$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-picard-lines** [claim](#stmt-pre-picard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-picard): Torsion and exponential sequence, bimeromorphic invariance of the identity component, projection formula. -> zero-class lines lie in the rationalised identity component; semiampleness descends
- **step-ordinary-input** [claim](#stmt-pre-ordinary) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Cited ordinary divisorial decomposition with the logarithmic Iitaka hypothesis supplied. -> semiample part plus negative part on a modification
- **step-contraction-input** [claim](#stmt-pre-contract) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Cited ordinary scaling program driven by detected analytic rays. -> klt model with semiample adjoint
- **step-projective-input** [claim](#stmt-pre-projective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-projective): Combine the cited positive-part theorem with a generalized klt program and compare on a common resolution. -> nef plus exceptional decomposition
- **step-numerical-descent** [claim](#stmt-desc-nef) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-nef): Flattening, push-forward current, intersection matrix argument on vertical divisors, relative negativity, rationality via injective pullback. -> numerical descent to a modified base
- **step-representative** [claim](#stmt-desc-representative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-representative): Flat twist by a rational element of the identity component of the Picard group. -> actual rational line identity
- **step-flat-descent** [claim](#stmt-desc-flat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-flat): Homotopy sequence, finite meridian holonomy, common power to descend the character. -> flat line from the base
- **step-period-input** [claim](#stmt-adj-period-input) / [proof](#pm-h-12): Cited period results for parabolic extensions of highest Hodge lines. -> nef line with descent to a quotient
- **step-root-eigenline** [claim](#stmt-adj-root) / [proof](#proof-stmt-adj-root): Cyclic root cover without a meromorphic frame, functorial resolution, averaged polarization, order computation for the root form. -> variation with a rank-one highest Hodge piece
- **step-extension** [claim](#stmt-adj-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-adj-extension): Hodge norm asymptotics near the discriminant and nilpotent-orbit estimates. -> parabolic extension equals the moduli line
- **step-rank-equality** [claim](#stmt-adj-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-adj-rank): Lift kernel vector fields through the cover using vanishing of an extension class, then use functorial resolution. -> equal generic period ranks
- **step-adjoint-base** [claim](#stmt-adj-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-adj-rank-2): Kodaira's lemma and Bertini perturbation on the nef quotient line, then push the divisor down. -> klt boundary on the base
- **step-mass-test** [claim](#stmt-zero-mass-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-mass-test): Intersection identity with a pseudo-effective difference and nonnegativity of mixed products. -> vanishing of a nef class
- **step-geometric-reduction** [claim](#stmt-zero-geometric-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-geometric-reduction): Ordinary decomposition, structure of Calabi–Yau type pairs, product intersection argument. -> reduction to simple symplectic manifolds
- **step-no-correspondences** [claim](#stmt-zero-no-correspondences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-no-correspondences): A sweeping correspondence family would give a holomorphic one-form on a fixed finite cover. -> only factor slices through a very general point
- **step-cotangent-slope** [claim](#stmt-zero-cotangent-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-cotangent-slope): Harder–Narasimhan slopes for movable classes, then countable evaluation injectivity for the blowup. -> slope bounds on line subsheaves
- **step-pole-threshold** [claim](#stmt-zero-pole-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-pole-threshold): Concavity of the root of the volume and the divisorial derivative formula. -> point pole bound
- **step-bundle-subvarieties** [claim](#stmt-zero-subvarieties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-subvarieties): Classify multisections by monomial coefficients; two coefficients give a meromorphic section of a power of the line. -> list of subvarieties through a very general point
- **step-two-slot-volume** [claim](#stmt-zero-two-slot-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-two-slot-volume): Fujita model with explicit Kähler part, lower bounds by mixed intersections, upper bound by integrating the derivative of volume. -> two-sided volume and pole bounds
- **step-nef-pushforward** [claim](#stmt-zero-nef-pushforward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-nef-pushforward): Flattening, zero Lelong numbers of the pushforward current, Demailly regularization, relative negativity. -> nef replacement of the pushforward class
- **step-direct-image** [claim](#stmt-zero-direct-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-direct-image): Relative Serre vanishing, Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch, log-concavity of mixed intersections. -> rank asymptotics and volume bound
- **step-slope-descent** [claim](#stmt-zero-slope-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-slope-descent): Fujita decomposition and the determinant of direct images mapped into tensor powers. -> descent of pseudo-effectivity from the bundle to the base
- **step-cotangent-z** [claim](#stmt-zero-cotangent-z) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-cotangent-z): Künneth and projective bundle formula, filtration of the cotangent bundle, graded components. -> degree bounds for lines in cotangent tensors on Z
- **step-diagonal-mass** [claim](#stmt-zero-diagonal-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-diagonal-mass): Integrate restricted volumes over the scale, discard small scales, choose a scale with large restricted volume. -> scale and current with a definite rank fraction
- **step-incidence-pole** [claim](#stmt-zero-incidence-pole) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-incidence-pole): Remove the generic pole, restrict to the exceptional divisor, apply slope descent to the conormal filtration, then the point bound. -> lower bound for the incidence pole
- **step-determinant-order** [claim](#stmt-zero-determinant-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-determinant-order): Local coefficient order along the exceptional divisor, monomial counting against the rank fraction, bound on maximal minors. -> common vanishing order of the determinant map
- **step-line-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-zero-line-nonvanishing) / [proof](#pm-h-19): Contradiction: large determinant vanishing against slope inequalities gives an impossible point pole. -> nonzero meromorphic section of a power
- **step-simple-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-zero-simple-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-simple-vanishing): Case of positive irregularity via the Albanese map; otherwise meromorphic nonvanishing and Boucksom decomposition for signed divisors. -> zero class for nef lines on simple symplectic manifolds
- **step-zero-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-zero-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-zero-simple-vanishing-2): Reduction lemma plus the simple case. -> vanishing in algebraic dimension zero
- **step-cycle-sections** [claim](#stmt-cycle-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cycle-sections): Coherent base change, ample twist, lifting sections to a meromorphic function, Baire's theorem. -> nonpositive fiberwise Iitaka dimension for a countable family
- **step-cycle-divisors** [claim](#stmt-cycle-divisors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cycle-divisors): Flat twist, analytic family of divisors from the very ample base line, containment of negative part. -> divisors distinguishing fibers with constant support
- **step-cycle-detection** [claim](#stmt-cycle-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cycle-detection): Relative divisor cycle spaces, incidence maps, flattening, countably many diagrams. -> countable collection of detecting fibrations
- **step-cycle-nef-summand** [claim](#stmt-cycle-nef-summand) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cycle-nef-summand): Fix global twists before choosing the fiber, then contradict the sections lemma through positive Iitaka dimension. -> zero class of the nef summand on a general fiber
- **step-cycle-base-descent** [claim](#stmt-cycle-base-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cycle-base-descent): Apply numerical descent with the fiberwise vanishing. -> nef rational line on a projective base
- **step-exceptional-negative-part** [claim](#stmt-finish-exceptional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-finish-exceptional): Bilinear form from mixed Hodge–Riemann relations, negative semidefiniteness on the orthogonal complement, contradiction with a positive pairing. -> exceptional divisor equals the negative part
- **step-program-over-base** [claim](#stmt-finish-over-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-finish-over-base): Add a large multiple of an ample line from the base, run the program, and show each ray is trivial against it by integrality. -> semiample augmented adjoint over the base
- **step-decomposition-induction** [claim](#stmt-thm-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-finish-over-base-2): Simultaneous induction on dimension: projective, zero algebraic dimension and intermediate cases. -> decomposition and vanishing in the next dimension
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-finish-over-base-3): For nef D the negative part vanishes; correct by a flat rational line and descend semiampleness. -> numerically semiample representative on X

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-numerical-semiample-representative**: Under the stated Kähler klt hypotheses, the nef adjoint class has the same real Bott–Chern class as some semiample rational line bundle. The conclusion is numerical: the original adjoint is not claimed to be semiample.
- **inv-vanishing-nef-summand-dimension-zero**: In the decomposition theorem, when the algebraic dimension of T is zero, the first Chern class of the nef summand M vanishes.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-original-adjoint-semiample**: Conclude that the original adjoint bundle itself has sections in some positive multiple. -> The theorem only provides a semiample line with the same real Chern class; the two may differ by a non-torsion flat line, so the original bundle can lack sections in every positive power.
- **anti-boundary-coefficients**: Apply the result to a pair whose boundary has a coefficient equal to or above one. -> The klt condition on a snc boundary is exactly that all coefficients are below one; the inductive arguments rely on it.
- **anti-non-kahler**: Use the statement on a compact complex manifold that is not Kähler. -> The setting uses Bott–Chern classes and Kähler-cone nefness, which are not available outside the Kähler case.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence theorems in complex analytic geometry: they assert a semiample rational line bundle or a divisorial decomposition exists, via induction, currents, cycle spaces and minimal model steps on compact Kähler manifolds. No finite procedure with explicit inputs and outputs is given, so there is no executable construction to test.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main results are existence statements for rational line bundles and divisor decompositions on compact Kähler manifolds. No finite procedure with executable inputs and outputs is given, so a library built from this paper has no specification. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no dynamics, discretisation or numerical model that a simulation could reproduce. The objects are cohomology classes and currents on abstract complex manifolds. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no algorithm that a checker or converter could implement. Its induction on dimension rests on cited black-box inputs, so it cannot be turned into a verification utility. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The minimal model program steps are used only as existence tools, after resolutions and modifications whose effective forms are not provided. No input-to-output procedure with a bounded cost exists in the text. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is defined or analysed. The objects are classes in Bott–Chern cohomology, divisors and sheaves, which are not representations with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper is a computation, dataflow or arithmetic kernel that hardware could speed up. The arguments are analytic and cohomological. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no hardness assumption, adversary, key or protocol. The finite-order and torsion phenomena it uses are geometric and carry no security meaning. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no data, loss, learning rule or generalisation statement. Positivity of currents and slopes of sheaves are not model-fitting notions. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, feasible set or solver appears. Suprema such as pole thresholds are used only to bound geometric quantities and are not an optimisation task. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no processes, messages, faults or consensus. The countable families of fibrations are a Baire-category device, not a protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only transferable value is conceptual, as a guide to proofs in complex and algebraic geometry. It gives no engineering construction, and the paper does not assert semiampleness of the original adjoint, only of a flat-twisted representative. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-numerical-only** (formal_scope): The conclusion is an equality of real Bott–Chern classes with a semiample rational line, reached after a flat change of line bundle. It does not assert semiampleness of the original adjoint. Blocked use: ['Concluding that the original adjoint has a nonzero section in some positive multiple', 'Treating the semiample representative as isomorphic to the original adjoint']
- **limit-klt-boundary** (hypothesis): The boundary is an effective rational simple normal crossing divisor whose coefficients are strictly less than one, which is the klt condition on a smooth model. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to a boundary with a coefficient equal to or above one', 'Applying it to a boundary with negative components']
- **limit-pseudo-effective-adjoint** (hypothesis): The theorem requires K_X+B pseudo-effective, M nef and the sum D nef; all three are used by the decomposition and by the vanishing of the negative part. Blocked use: ['Invoking the result when K_X+B is not pseudo-effective but the sum happens to be nef', 'Dropping nefness of M']
- **limit-kahler-setting** (hypothesis): The framework uses real Bott–Chern classes and the closure of the Kähler cone, relying on the ∂∂-bar lemma on compact Kähler manifolds. Blocked use: ['Using the statements on a compact complex manifold that is not Kähler']
- **limit-induction-dimension-zero** (formal_scope): The algebraic-dimension-zero vanishing is proved inside a simultaneous induction on dimension, so it is available in dimension n only given the assertion in lower dimensions. Blocked use: ['Using the vanishing of a nef class as a free-standing statement without the inductive hypothesis']
- **limit-cited-inputs** (nonconstructive): The proof rests on cited projective and ordinary Kähler inputs and on existence arguments (Baire category choices, cycle spaces, modifications) that give no effective construction. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or explicit bounds for the semiample line', 'Treating the program steps as computable procedures']
- **limit-general-fiber-vanishing** (edge_case): Vanishing of the nef summand is shown on a general smooth fiber of the algebraic reduction for intermediate algebraic dimension, not on every fiber. Blocked use: ['Claiming the restriction of M is numerically trivial on singular fibers']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>X</i> be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let <i>B</i> be an effective rational simple normal crossing divisor with coefficients less than one, and let <i>M</i> be a nef rational holomorphic line bundle on <i>X</i>. If $`K_X+B`$ is pseudo-effective and $`K_X+B+M`$ is nef, we prove that its first Chern class in real Bott–Chern cohomology is represented by a semiample rational line bundle. This numerical statement allows a flat change of line bundle; it does not assert semiampleness of the original adjoint.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| [stmt-pre-picard](#stmt-pre-picard) | lemma | `pre:picard` | 3 | Let $\mu:Y\to X$ be a modification between smooth compact Kähler manifolds.* |
| [stmt-pre-negative](#stmt-pre-negative) | lemma | `pre:negative` | 4 | Let $\alpha$ be pseudo-effective on a smooth compact Kähler manifold.* |
| [stmt-pre-ordinary](#stmt-pre-ordinary) | theorem | `pre:ordinary` | 5 | Let $X$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold, and let $B$ be a rational simple normal crossing divisor with coefficients in $[0,1]$. |
| [stmt-pre-contract](#stmt-pre-contract) | proposition | `pre:contract` | 6 | Let $(X,B)$ be a smooth compact Kähler klt pair with rational simple normal crossing boundary. |
| [stmt-pre-projective](#stmt-pre-projective) | proposition | `pre:projective` | 7 | Let $(S,\Delta)$ be a normal projective klt pair with rational boundary, and let $M$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A central aim of abundance theory is to recover a holomorphic map from the positivity of an adjoint class. For an ordinary log canonical line, the expected conclusion is semiampleness of that line itself. Adding an arbitrary nef line introduces a different phenomenon: its flat part need not be torsion. The natural conclusion is then numerical semiampleness, namely the existence of a semiample line with the same real Chern class.

We prove this statement for smooth compact Kähler manifolds with a klt boundary. A *rational line bundle* is an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$. It is nef when its first Chern class belongs to the closure of the Kähler cone, and pseudo-effective when that class contains a closed positive $(1,1)$-current. It is semiample when a positive integral multiple is a holomorphic line bundle generated everywhere by global sections.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold, let $B\geq0$ be a rational simple normal crossing divisor whose coefficients are strictly less than one, and let $M\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$. Assume that $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, $M$ is nef, and $D=K_X+B+M$ is nef. Then there is a semiample rational line bundle $L$ on $X$ such that $$c_1(L)=c_1(D)\quad\text{in }H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(X,\mathbb R).$$*

</div>

The numerical formulation is essential even on an elliptic curve: a nontorsion degree-zero line $M$ is nef but has no section in any positive power, whereas its Chern class is represented by the trivial line. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> allows exactly this change of flat part. It keeps the nef line $M$ on the original manifold; it is not a statement about an arbitrary nef b-divisor on a higher model.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and the inputs to the proof

Lazić and Peternell formulated generalized abundance for projective klt pairs as precisely this numerical semiampleness problem (**LP1?**, Generalised Abundance Conjecture). Their work relates it to ordinary abundance and to positivity on varieties with numerically trivial canonical class, using nef reduction and the positive part of the divisorial Zariski decomposition (**LP1?**; **LP2?**). They proved the surface case and the threefold case in which the ordinary adjoint has positive numerical dimension (**LP1?**, Corollaries C and D). The present proof uses the projective positive-part theorem of (OpenAI 2026b, Proposition 8.1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[P](#ref-P)] as an input. Our task is to pass from that projective statement to the analytic setting, where the nef summand need not be represented by a divisor and a manifold may have no nonconstant meromorphic functions.

The compact Kähler minimal model program has developed alongside these abundance questions.
